markup
words–
SEno markup
se-llm<section epub:type="dedication">
se-llm step: Name and order sections
SE correct section type
Gutenberg does have a dedication to the Sierra Club, so a dedication section is defensible. But se-llm nests the frontispiece figure inside the dedication section, and SE's manual treats the frontispiece as its own section, not part of a dedication. SE's separate frontispiece section is the cleaner structure.
markup
words–
SE<img epub:type="z3998:photograph">
se-llm<img epub:type="z3998:illustration">
se-llm step: Number illustrations
SE correct image semantics
The frontispiece is a photograph. Gutenberg's list of illustrations credits it as 'From a photograph by Charles S. Olcott'. z3998:photograph is the right epub:type, and z3998:illustration is too generic.

se-llm kept the Gutenberg reading; SE changed it
se-llm step: kept from the Gutenberg draft
SE edition difference caption rewrite
SE rewrote the frontispiece caption (dropping "The", adding a period) and moved the front matter. se-llm keeps the Gutenberg caption text.

<h2><a id="ILLUSTRATIONS"></a>ILLUSTRATIONS</h2>
<table style="border-spacing: 2px;padding: 2px;border-width: 0px;width: 100%;" class="xhtml_table_width" data-summary="CONTENTS">
<tbody><tr>
<td style="text-align: left;"><span class="smcap">The Yosemite Falls, Yosemite National Park</span></td>
<td style="text-align: right;"><a href="#Frontispiece" class="pginternal"><i>Frontispiece</i></a></td></tr>
</tbody></table>
<div class="blockquot"><p>The total height of the three falls is 2600 feet. The upper fall is
about 1600 feet, and the lower about 400 feet. Mr. Muir was
probably the only man who ever looked down into the heart of the
fall from the narrow ledge of rocks near the top.</p>
<span style="margin-left: 2em;"><i>From a photograph by Charles S. Olcott</i></span><br></div>
<table style="border-spacing: 2px;padding: 2px;border-width: 0px;width: 100%;" class="xhtml_table_width" data-summary="CONTENTS">
<tbody><tr>
<td style="text-align: left;"><span class="smcap">Sheep in the Mountains</span></td>
<td style="text-align: right;"><a href="#photo1" class="pginternal">8</a></td></tr>
</tbody></table>
<div class="blockquot"><p>Since the establishment of the Yosemite National Park the pasturing
of sheep has not been allowed within its boundaries, and as a
result the grasses and wild flowers have recovered very much of
their former luxuriance. The flock of sheep here photographed were
feeding near Alger Lake on the slope of Blacktop Mountain, at an
altitude of about 10,000 feet and just beyond the eastern boundary
of the Park.</p>
<span style="margin-left: 2em;"><i>From a photograph by Herbert W. Gleason</i></span><br></div>
<table style="border-spacing: 2px;padding: 2px;border-width: 0px;width: 100%;" class="xhtml_table_width" data-summary="CONTENTS">
<tbody><tr>
<td style="text-align: left;"><span class="smcap">A Silver Fir, or Red Fir</span> (<i>Abies magnifica</i>)</td>
<td style="text-align: right;"><a href="#photo2" class="pginternal">90</a></td></tr>
</tbody></table>
<div class="blockquot"><p>This tree was found in an extensive forest of red fir above the
Middle Fork of King’s River. It was estimated to be about 250 feet
high. Mr. Muir, on being shown the photograph, remarked that it was
one of the finest and most mature specimens of the red fir that he
had ever seen.</p>
<span style="margin-left: 2em;"><i>From a photograph by Herbert W. Gleason</i></span><br></div>
<table style="border-spacing: 2px;padding: 2px;border-width: 0px;width: 100%;" class="xhtml_table_width" data-summary="CONTENTS">

<td style="text-align: right;"><a href="#photo3" class="pginternal">122</a></td></tr>
</tbody></table>
<div class="blockquot"><p>The great rock on the right is the South Dome, commonly called the
Half-Dome, according to Mr. Muir “the most beautiful and most
sublime of all the Yosemite rocks.” The one on the left is the
North Dome, while in the center is the Washington Column.</p>
<span style="margin-left: 2em;"><i>From a photograph by Charles S. Olcott</i></span><br></div>

SE's manual marks dated journal entries as a z3998:diary section holding blockquotes with z3998:diary-entry, each with a se:diary.dateline paragraph. se-llm kept the Gutenberg <hr/> and italic date. se-llm step: se-llm has no markup here
SE correct section type
SE closes the diary section and diary-entry blockquote that wrap the chapter's dated entries, following the SE diary structure (z3998:diary / z3998:diary-entry). Gutenberg has only plain paragraphs with italic dates, and se-llm never added the diary wrapper, so there is nothing to close.

se-llm correct body epub:type
The book is a non-fiction memoir and journal. Under the SE semantics rules, body epub:type should carry z3998:non-fiction, not z3998:fiction. SE's 'fiction' is a mis-tag.

words–
SE</blockquote>
se-llm<hr>
se-llm step: Record fresh draft
SE correct scene break (hr)
In the SE manual, <hr/> marks scene or section breaks in running text. Here the Gutenberg rules just frame the map plate after "THE END", so se-llm's <hr/> is leftover decoration with no semantic meaning. SE drops them and uses a plain figure.
text
Gutenbergnothing

se-llm kept the Gutenberg reading; SE changed it
se-llm step: kept from the Gutenberg draft
SE edition difference dropped text
SE drops the book's index, which is back matter. It replaces the map's image with a descriptive caption. se-llm keeps the index, with Cañon changed to Canyon and ranges given en dashes.

SE correct scene break (hr)
The <hr/> elements are carried over from Gutenberg's decorative rules around the map image and are not true thematic breaks under the SE manual. SE ends the section cleanly with the figure and no <hr/>.
